<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For normal projective log canonical pairs over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero, of fixed dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 5 and with effective boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set, we prove that one complete rounded pluricanonical system generates the full Iitaka field whenever the ℚ-Cartier log canonical divisor has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. Its degree depends only on the dimension and coefficient set. For the paper's normalized canonical bundle formulae over ℂ, we also bound the Cartier denominators of the moduli divisors on smooth projective determining models in terms of the dimension and coefficient set.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d\geq5$ and every finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $m=m(d,\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $k$ be any algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B)$ be a normal integral projective lc $d$-fold pair over $k$. Assume that $B\geq0$, every nonzero coefficient of $B$ belongs to $\Phi$, $D=K_X+B$ is rational Cartier, and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$. Then $$V_m(D)\neq0,\qquad F_m(D)=K(D) \quad\text{inside }k(X).$$
  - Explanation: A uniform rounded pluricanonical degree generates the entire Iitaka field under the stated log canonical, coefficient, dimension and ground-field hypotheses.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-normal-index)): For every integer $n\geq0$ and every DCC set $\Psi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $a(n,\Psi)>0$ such that every normal integral projective lc pair $(V,\Delta)$ over $\mathbb C$ with $\dim V=n$, $\Delta\geq0$, coefficients in $\Psi$, and $K_V+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ satisfies $$a(n,\Psi)(K_V+\Delta)\sim0.$$ Here the displayed multiple is an integral principal divisor.
  - Explanation: The companion index theorem principalizes log Calabi–Yau adjoints in a uniform degree for the stated coefficient set.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-good-models)): Every projective lc pair $(V,\Delta)$ over $\mathbb C$ with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoint $K_V+\Delta$ has a good log minimal model.
  - Explanation: The companion good-model theorem supplies a good log minimal model for the stated pseudo-effective adjoint.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-stein-degree)): For every integer $n\geq1$ and every real number $t>0$, there is an integer $N(n,t)$ with the following property. Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, and let $(V,\Delta)$ be a normal integral projective lc rational pair over $F$ with $$\dim V=n,\qquad H^0(V,\mathcal O_V)=F,\qquad \Delta\geq0,\qquad K_V+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0.$$ If $S$ is a prime component of $\Delta$ of coefficient at least $t$, the algebraic closure of $F$ in $F(S)$ has degree at most $N(n,t)$ over $F$.
  - Explanation: The companion arithmetic theorem bounds new constants in a boundary component with a prescribed positive coefficient threshold.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trivialization-descent)): Let $V$ be a geometrically integral normal projective variety over a characteristic-zero field $F$. Let $D$ be an integral Weil divisor. If $D_{\overline F}$ is principal, then $D$ is principal. In particular, an integral principal multiple of a rational log canonical divisor on $V_{\overline F}$ descends in the same degree to $V$.
  - Explanation: Principality descends from the algebraic closure without increasing the degree of the integral divisor.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-mmp)): Let $f:X\to C$ be a contraction from a projective variety over $\mathbb C$ to a smooth projective curve, and let $(X,\Delta)$ be a $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair with effective rational boundary. Suppose that, for some sufficiently divisible integer $r>0$, $$H^0\bigl(X_\eta,\mathcal O_{X_\eta}(r(K_{X_\eta}+\Delta_\eta))\bigr)\ne0, \qquad \eta=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathbb C(C).$$ There is a $(K_X+\Delta)$-MMP over $C$ that terminates with a $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair $(X',\Delta')$ for which $K_{X'}+\Delta'$ is semiample over $C$. If $(X,\Delta)$ is klt, its output is klt.
  - Explanation: A generic adjoint section permits a terminating relative minimal-model program with semiample output over the curve.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-weight)): For integers $s\ge0$ and $m\ge1$ there is an integer $q(s,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, $c\in C$, and $z$ a rational uniformizer at $c$. Let $V$ be a normal geometrically integral projective $s$-fold over $K=\mathbb C(C)$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically log canonical pair with effective rational boundary. If a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\phi$ satisfies $$\label{eq:weight-log-trivialization} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_V(\phi)+mB=0,$$ then $$q(s,m)w_z(\phi)\in\mathbb Z.$$ The integer $q(s,m)$ is independent of the boundary coefficients and of the curve, variety, and form.
  - Explanation: Relative dimension and log-trivialization degree alone control the denominator of the curve weight.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weight-rules)): Weights of rational relative pluriforms have the following properties.* 1. *They do not depend on the rational uniformizer at the fixed point. For $a\in K^*$ and $t\ge1$, $$w_z(a\phi)=w_z(\phi)+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_c a}{m}, \qquad w_z(\phi^{\otimes t})=w_z(\phi).$$* 2. *If $F'/F$ is finite and $K$ is algebraically closed in $F'$, then the pullback of $\phi$ has the same weight over $C$.* 3. *Let $K'/K$ be finite Galois, let $C'\to C$ be the associated map of smooth projective curves, and let $c'\in C'$ lie above $c$ with ramification index $l$. For a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$ and the pullback $\phi'$ to $FK'/K'$, one has $$\label{eq:weight-base-change} w_u(\phi')=l w_z(\phi).$$
  - Explanation: Weights obey scalar, tensor-power, finite-cover and ramified curve-change rules.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-snc-weight)): Let $f:W\to C$ be a projective model of $F/K$ with $W$ smooth. Put $F_W=f^*c$ and $T_W=(F_W)_{\mathrm{red}}$. Let $H_W$ be an effective horizontal rational boundary whose coefficients are at most one, and suppose that $T_W+H_W$ has simple normal crossings near $T_W$. For $\Omega=\phi\wedge(dz/z)^{\otimes m}$, assume that the horizontal part of $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_W(\Omega)/m+H_W$ is effective. Then $$\label{eq:weight-snc-minimum} w_z(\phi)=\min_{D\subset T_W} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_D(\Omega)+m}{m\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_D z},$$ where $D$ ranges over the prime components of $T_W$.
  - Explanation: On the stated log-smooth model the weight is attained among the central fibre components.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-prepared)): For the data of Theorem <a href="#thm:weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:weight">8</a>, with $s>0$, there is a projective contraction $f:N\to C$ and an effective horizontal rational boundary $H$ such that:* 1. *$N$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial, its generic fibre is geometrically integral and birational to $V$, and $(N,T+H)$ is dlt, where $T=(f^*c)_{\mathrm{red}}$;* 2. *$K_N+T+H\sim_{\mathbb Q,C}0$;* 3. *for the same field-theoretic form $\Omega$ and $w=w_z(\phi)$, there is an equality of rational divisors near $T$, $$\label{eq:prepared-equality} \frac1m\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_N(\Omega)+T+H=w f^*c.$$* *In particular, on the generic fibre, $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\phi)+mH_\eta=0$. If $H$ has no coefficient-one component, then $(N_\eta,H_\eta)$ is geometrically klt and $K_{N_\eta}$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier.
  - Explanation: A relative minimal model makes the weight an exact coefficient in a divisor identity.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residue-reduction)): Let $f:(N,T+H)\to C$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:prepared" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:prepared">11</a>, with relative dimension $s\ge1$, and suppose that $S$ is a coefficient-one component of $H$. Factor $S\to C$ as $$S\xrightarrow{h}C_S\xrightarrow{\nu}C,$$ where $h$ is a contraction and $\nu$ is finite. Then $C_S$ is a smooth projective curve and $$\deg\nu\le D_s$$ for an integer $D_s$ depending only on $s$. For every $c'\in C_S$ above $c$, with ramification index $e$, there are data satisfying Theorem <a href="#thm:weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:weight">8</a> in relative dimension $s-1$ and degree $m$ over $\mathbb C(C_S)$, with a rational form $\phi_S$ whose weight is $$\label{eq:residue-weight} w_u(\phi_S)=e w_z(\phi)$$ for a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$.
  - Explanation: Residue along horizontal coefficient-one boundary reduces fibre dimension with bounded new curve constants.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-product-cover)): Let $(V,B)$ be a projective klt pair over $\mathbb C$ with $B\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. There is a finite quasi-étale cover $\pi:P\to V$ and a decomposition $$(P,\pi^*B) \simeq (F,H)\times A\times\prod_j Y_j\times\prod_k Z_k,$$ where $(F,H)$ is a rationally connected klt log Calabi–Yau pair, $A$ is an abelian variety, and the $Y_j$ and $Z_k$ are respectively irreducible Calabi–Yau and irreducible holomorphic symplectic varieties in the singular Beauville–Bogomolov decomposition. All factors are $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. Point factors are omitted, and the boundary is pulled back entirely from $F$.
  - Explanation: A finite quasi-étale cover separates the boundary-bearing rationally connected factor from the boundary-free factors.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rc-aut)): Let $(F,H)$ be a rationally connected projective klt pair over $\mathbb C$ with $H\geq0$ and $K_F+H\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. Then $$H^j(F,\mathcal O_F)=0\quad(j>0),\qquad H^0(F,\Omega_F^{[1]})=0,\qquad H^0\bigl(F,\mathcal T_F(-\log\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}H)\bigr)=0.$$ For every ample line bundle $L$, the group of automorphisms of $(F,H)$ preserving $L$ is finite.
  - Explanation: The rationally connected log Calabi–Yau factor has the stated cohomological and tangent-field vanishings and finite polarized automorphisms.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-minimal-rc)): The cover in Proposition <a href="#prop:product-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:product-cover">13</a> can be chosen so that every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of $F$ is rationally connected. On every such cover, with the pulled-back boundary, the conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:rc-aut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rc-aut">14</a> hold.
  - Explanation: A minimal choice of rationally connected block remains rationally connected under further quasi-étale covers.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-semilinear)): Let $k$ be a characteristic-zero field, and let $P=\prod_{i\in I}P_i$ be a product of geometrically integral projective blocks as above. Let $H_P$ be the pullback of the boundary on its rationally connected block, or zero if that block is absent. Suppose $$\rho:R\longrightarrow P$$ is a finite quasi-étale morphism from a geometrically integral normal variety, and a finite group $G$ acts semilinearly on $(R,H_R)$, where $H_R=\rho^*H_P$. Put $s=\dim R>0$. There are normal geometrically integral varieties $R_i$, finite quasi-étale maps $R_i\to P_i$, and a finite quasi-étale map $R\to\prod_iR_i$ such that every $g^{s!}$, for $g\in G$, induces regular semilinear automorphisms of the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. Here $H_i$ is the pulled-back boundary on the rationally connected block and is zero on the other blocks. The projections from $R$ are equivariant for these automorphisms. Each geometric $R_i$ has the same block properties as $P_i$.
  - Explanation: A bounded power of the finite semilinear action induces regular actions on finite covers of the individual blocks.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-block-forms)): Let $K=\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective curve $C$. Let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective $s$-fold pair over $K$, with $s>0$, geometrically klt, $B\geq0$, and $K_V$ rational Cartier. Suppose that, for an integer $m>0$, a rational $m$-canonical form $\phi$ satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\phi)+mB=0$.* *There is a finite Galois extension $K_1/K$, a geometrically integral normal projective variety $R/K_1$, and finite quasi-étale maps $$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large] R \arrow[r] & \displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}R_i \arrow[r] & \displaystyle\prod_{i\in I}P_i \arrow[r] & V_{K_1} \end{tikzcd}$$ with the following properties. The field $K_1(R)$ is finite Galois over $K(V)$; its Galois group acts semilinearly on $R$, maps onto $\operatorname{Gal}(K_1/K)$, and preserves the pulled-back pair. For each element $g$ of this group, $g^{s!}$ induces regular semilinear actions on the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. There is at most one rationally connected block, chosen with the cover-stability of Lemma <a href="#lem:minimal-rc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:minimal-rc">15</a>, and all other blocks are boundary-free of the types in Proposition <a href="#prop:product-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:product-cover">13</a>.* *Put $p_i=m$ on the rationally connected block and $p_i=1$ on the other blocks. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\eta_i$ over $K_1$ satisfying $$\label{eq:block-trivializations} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\eta_i)+p_iH_i=0.$$ On $R$, after pulling back all forms, one has $$\label{eq:block-product-form} \phi=a\bigwedge_{i\in I}\eta_i^{\otimes m/p_i} \qquad\text{for some }a\in K_1^*.$$ The wedge uses any fixed ordering of the blocks and the natural identification of their relative canonical lines.
  - Explanation: The comparison cover carries factor log trivializations whose product differs from the original form by a base scalar.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-marked-semistable)): Let $C_1$ be a smooth projective complex curve with a marked point $c_1$, and let a finite cyclic group act on $C_1$, fixing $c_1$. Consider finitely many geometrically integral normal projective varieties over $\mathbb C(C_1)$, each with a compatible semilinear action of this group and an invariant finite set of horizontal divisor markings. After a finite extension of curve fields and replacement by compatible finite-order lifts of the actions, there are equivariant smooth projective models for which the marked fibre is reduced simple normal crossings and its union with the horizontal markings is simple normal crossings. The generic fibres may be log resolutions. If a given generic fibre is already smooth and has no markings requiring resolution, it can be left unchanged.
  - Explanation: Equivariant semistable reduction preserves horizontal markings after an unrestricted finite curve extension.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-product)): Multiplying each $\eta_i$ by an integral power of $u$, one can arrange $w_u(\eta_i)=0$. For these normalized forms, the exterior product in common degree $m$ has weight zero. On the comparison cover there is a base function $a\in K'^*$ such that $$\label{eq:product-weight} \phi=a\bigwedge_i\eta_i^{\otimes m/p_i}, \qquad l\,w_z(\phi)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{c'}a}{m}.$$ Here the exterior product denotes the tensor power of the relative canonical product identification, with a fixed order of factors.
  - Explanation: Uniformizer powers normalize factor weights, and the weight of their product vanishes.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-abelian-character)): For an abelian factor of dimension $h$, the order of its normalized character $\lambda_i$ is bounded in terms of $h$ alone.
  - Explanation: The normalized character of an abelian factor has order bounded by its dimension.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-equivariant-good)): Let $(R_i,H_i)$ be a rationally connected or nonabelian Beauville–Bogomolov factor of dimension $h>0$ above, with $p_i\in\{1,m\}$ and normalized form $\eta_i$. There is a projective equivariant model $Y\to C'$ such that, near $c'$,* 1. *$Y$ is klt and its fibre $T=Y_{c'}$ is reduced Cartier;* 2. *with $J$ the transformed horizontal boundary, $(Y,T+J)$ is dlt and $K_Y+J$ is semiample over $C'$;* 3. *for $\Omega_i=\eta_i\wedge(du/u)^{\otimes p_i}$, $$\label{eq:log-generator} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(\Omega_i)+p_i(T+J)=0.$$* *The geometric generic fibre is birational to $R_i$.
  - Explanation: An equivariant relative good model realizes the normalized form as an exact logarithmic generator.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-character)): Fix $h\geq0$ and $p\geq1$. Let $(A,\Delta)$ be a normal integral projective lc pair over $\mathbb C$, and let $\omega$ be a nonzero rational $p$-canonical form with $$\Delta\geq0,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\omega)+p\Delta=0.$$ If a finite-order automorphism $\gamma$ preserves the pair and $\gamma^*\omega=\lambda\omega$, the order of $\lambda$ divides an integer depending only on $h=\dim A$ and $p$.
  - Explanation: A finite character on an exact log trivialization is annihilated uniformly in dimension and form degree.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-other-characters)): For the normalized rationally connected, Calabi–Yau, and symplectic factors, the orders of the $\lambda_i$ are bounded in terms of $s$ and $m$.
  - Explanation: Normal special-fibre components and fixed-point arguments bound the remaining factor characters.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-klt-weight)): For every $s\geq1$ and $m\geq1$ there is a positive integer $q_{\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)$ with the following property. Let $K=\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective complex curve, let $z$ be a uniformizer at $c\in C$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective geometrically klt $s$-fold pair over $K$. Assume that $K_V$ is rational Cartier, $B\geq0$, and that a rational $m$-canonical form $\phi$ satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\phi)+mB=0$. Then $$q_{\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)w_z(\phi)\in\mathbb Z.$$
  - Explanation: Bounded factor characters cancel unrestricted ramification and clear the klt curve weight.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-presentation)): Fix an integer $d\geq 1$ and a finite set $\Phi\subset [0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$. There is an integer $p_0=p_0(d,\Phi)>0$, clearing the denominators of $\Phi$, with the following property. Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective varieties with $\dim X\leq d$, and let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\geq0$ and nonzero coefficients in $\Phi$. Suppose that $K_X+B$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $$K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*L$$ for a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$. Then there are $\psi\in\mathbb C(X)^*$ and a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D_Z\sim_{\mathbb Q}L$ such that $$\label{eq:exact-presentation} K_X+B+\frac{1}{p_0}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z.$$
  - Explanation: A uniform degree fixes an exact principal presentation of the log adjoint as a base pullback.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-moduli-denominator)): Fix $d\geq1$ and a finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, and let $p_0=p_0(d,\Phi)$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:exact-presentation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exact-presentation">25</a>. There is a positive integer $p=p(d,\Phi)$ divisible by $p_0$ with the following property. Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties with $\dim X\leq d$ and $\dim Z>0$, and let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\geq0$, coefficients in $\Phi$, and $K_X+B$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. For any exact presentation $$K_X+B+\frac1{p_0}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ with $\psi\in\mathbb C(X)^*$ and $D_Z$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, let $\mathbf M$ be its moduli b-divisor. On every smooth projective determination $W$ of $\mathbf M$, the divisor $pM_W$ is nef Cartier.
  - Explanation: The normalized presentation has a uniform nef Cartier multiple of the moduli trace on every smooth determination.
- **C26** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slice)): Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\geq0$, and suppose that $$K_X+B+\frac1m\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ for an integer $m>0$, a function $\psi\in\mathbb C(X)^*$, and a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D_Z$. Let $\tau:W\to Z$ be a smooth projective birational model, and let $P$ be a prime divisor of $W$. Write $\alpha=\operatorname{coeff}_P(\tau^*D_Z)$, and let $t_P$ be the threshold for the crepant induced sub-pair over the generic point of $P$. Put $s=\dim X-\dim Z$. Then there are a smooth projective curve $C$, a point $c\in C$, a rational uniformizer $z$ at $c$, and a regular function-field extension of $\mathbb C(C)$ admitting a geometrically integral projective normal effective $s$-fold pair $(V,B_V)$ that is geometrically lc, and a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\phi$ such that $$\label{eq:slice-weight} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\phi)+mB_V=0,\qquad w_z(\phi)=\alpha-1+t_P.$$
  - Explanation: A transverse curve realizes a moduli-coefficient expression as a weight with effective log canonical generic data.
- **C27** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rounded-comparison)): All varieties in this statement are normal and projective over a field of characteristic zero.* 1. *Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction and let $D$ and $D_Z$ be $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors such that $$D+\frac1a\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ for an integer $a>0$ and $\psi\in k(X)^*$. For every positive $\ell$ divisible by $a$, the spaces of divisorial sections, regarded as rational functions, satisfy $$\label{eq:section-pullback} H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)\bigr) =\psi^{\ell/a}f^*H^0\bigl(Z,\mathcal O_Z(\left\lfloor \ell D_Z\right\rfloor)\bigr).$$* 2. *Let $X\dashrightarrow X'$ be birational, let $D$ and $D'$ be $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors, and suppose that on a common resolution $p:T\to X$, $q:T\to X'$ one has $$p^*D=q^*D'+E,$$ where $E\geq0$ is $q$-exceptional. Under the identification of the function fields, for every integer $\ell>0$ one has $$\label{eq:section-birational} H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)\bigr) =H^0\bigl(X',\mathcal O_{X'}(\left\lfloor \ell D'\right\rfloor)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Complete rounded section spaces agree under the stated exact pullback and exceptional birational comparisons.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-effective-fibration)): Fix $d\geq1$ and a finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$. There is an integer $N=N(d,\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties with $\dim X\leq d$ and $\dim Z>0$, and let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\geq0$, coefficients in $\Phi$, and $D=K_X+B$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*L$ for a big $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$. Then $\|\left\lfloor ND\right\rfloor\|$ is nonempty, and its section ratios generate exactly $f^*\mathbb C(Z)$ inside $\mathbb C(X)$. This is also the field $K(D)$ generated by section ratios in all positive degrees.
  - Explanation: A uniform rounded system generates exactly the base field and the full adjoint ratio field.
- **C29** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-field-descent)): Let $k\subset k'$ be an extension of algebraically closed fields, let $X$ be a normal integral projective variety over $k$, and let $D$ be a $\mathbb Q$-divisor on $X$. Write $X'=X_{k'}$ and $D'=D_{k'}$. For each $\ell>0$ having nonzero sections, let $F_\ell\subset k(X)$ be the field generated over $k$ by ratios of sections of $\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)$, and define $F'_\ell$ similarly on $X'$. Then $$F'_\ell=k'F_\ell,\qquad K(D')=k'K(D),$$ where the composita are taken in $k'(X')$. In particular, for a fixed $m>0$, nonemptiness of $\|\left\lfloor mD\right\rfloor\|$ and the equality $F_m=K(D)$ hold if and only if they hold after extension to $k'$.
  - Explanation: Nonemptiness and equality of ratio fields ascend and descend under algebraically closed field extensions.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-mmp",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to C$ be a contraction from a projective variety over $\\mathbb C$ to a smooth projective curve, and let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair with effective rational boundary. Suppose that, for some sufficiently divisible integer $r>0$, $$H^0\\bigl(X_\\eta,\\mathcal O_{X_\\eta}(r(K_{X_\\eta}+\\Delta_\\eta))\\bigr)\\ne0,\n \\qquad \\eta=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\mathbb C(C).$$ There is a $(K_X+\\Delta)$-MMP over $C$ that terminates with a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair $(X',\\Delta')$ for which $K_{X'}+\\Delta'$ is semiample over $C$. If $(X,\\Delta)$ is klt, its output is klt.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-weight",
        "exact_quote": "For integers $s\\ge0$ and $m\\ge1$ there is an integer $q(s,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, $c\\in C$, and $z$ a rational uniformizer at $c$. Let $V$ be a normal geometrically integral projective $s$-fold over $K=\\mathbb C(C)$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically log canonical pair with effective rational boundary. If a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:weight-log-trivialization}\n                         \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_V(\\phi)+mB=0,$$ then $$q(s,m)w_z(\\phi)\\in\\mathbb Z.$$ The integer $q(s,m)$ is independent of the boundary coefficients and of the curve, variety, and form.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-weight",
          "exact_quote": "For integers $s\\ge0$ and $m\\ge1$ there is an integer $q(s,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, $c\\in C$, and $z$ a rational uniformizer at $c$. Let $V$ be a normal geometrically integral projective $s$-fold over $K=\\mathbb C(C)$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically log canonical pair with effective rational boundary. If a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:weight-log-trivialization}\n                         \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_V(\\phi)+mB=0,$$ then $$q(s,m)w_z(\\phi)\\in\\mathbb Z.$$ The integer $q(s,m)$ is independent of the boundary coefficients and of the curve, variety, and form.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For integers $s\\ge0$ and $m\\ge1$ there is an integer $q(s,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal geometrically integral projective $s$-fold over $K=\\mathbb C(C)$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically log canonical pair with effective rational boundary. If a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:weight-log-trivialization}\n                         \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_V(\\phi)+mB=0,$$ then $$q(s,m)w_z(\\phi)\\in\\mathbb Z.$$ The integer $q(s,m)$ is independent of the boundary coefficients and of the curve, variety, and form.",
      "explanation": "Relative dimension and log-trivialization degree alone control the denominator of the curve weight.",

          "source_id": "PM-034.9"

          "exact_quote": "For integers $s\\ge0$ and $m\\ge1$ there is an integer $q(s,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal geometrically integral projective $s$-fold over $K=\\mathbb C(C)$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically log canonical pair with effective rational boundary.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-rules",
        "exact_quote": "Weights of rational relative pluriforms have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *They do not depend on the rational uniformizer at the fixed point. For $a\\in K^*$ and $t\\ge1$, $$w_z(a\\phi)=w_z(\\phi)+\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_c a}{m},\n     \\qquad w_z(\\phi^{\\otimes t})=w_z(\\phi).$$*\n\n2.  *If $F'/F$ is finite and $K$ is algebraically closed in $F'$, then the pullback of $\\phi$ has the same weight over $C$.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $K'/K$ be finite Galois, let $C'\\to C$ be the associated map of smooth projective curves, and let $c'\\in C'$ lie above $c$ with ramification index $l$. For a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$ and the pullback $\\phi'$ to $FK'/K'$, one has $$\\label{eq:weight-base-change}\n                                  w_u(\\phi')=l w_z(\\phi).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-weight-rules",

For a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$ and the pullback $\\phi'$ to $FK'/K'$, one has $$\\label{eq:weight-base-change}\n                                  w_u(\\phi')=l w_z(\\phi).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-snc-weight",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:W\\to C$ be a projective model of $F/K$ with $W$ smooth. Put $F_W=f^*c$ and $T_W=(F_W)_{\\mathrm{red}}$. Let $H_W$ be an effective horizontal rational boundary whose coefficients are at most one, and suppose that $T_W+H_W$ has simple normal crossings near $T_W$. For $\\Omega=\\phi\\wedge(dz/z)^{\\otimes m}$, assume that the horizontal part of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_W(\\Omega)/m+H_W$ is effective. Then $$\\label{eq:weight-snc-minimum}\n w_z(\\phi)=\\min_{D\\subset T_W}\n                \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_D(\\Omega)+m}{m\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_D z},$$ where $D$ ranges over the prime components of $T_W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Then $$\\label{eq:weight-snc-minimum}\n w_z(\\phi)=\\min_{D\\subset T_W}\n                \\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_D(\\Omega)+m}{m\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_D z},$$ where $D$ ranges over the prime components of $T_W$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prepared",
        "exact_quote": "For the data of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:weight\">8</a>, with $s>0$, there is a projective contraction $f:N\\to C$ and an effective horizontal rational boundary $H$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$N$ is $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, its generic fibre is geometrically integral and birational to $V$, and $(N,T+H)$ is dlt, where $T=(f^*c)_{\\mathrm{red}}$;*\n\n2.  *$K_N+T+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q,C}0$;*\n\n\n3.  *for the same field-theoretic form $\\Omega$ and $w=w_z(\\phi)$, there is an equality of rational divisors near $T$, $$\\label{eq:prepared-equality}\n                        \\frac1m\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_N(\\Omega)+T+H=w f^*c.$$*\n\n*In particular, on the generic fibre, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mH_\\eta=0$. If $H$ has no coefficient-one component, then $(N_\\eta,H_\\eta)$ is geometrically klt and $K_{N_\\eta}$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [

If $H$ has no coefficient-one component, then $(N_\\eta,H_\\eta)$ is geometrically klt and $K_{N_\\eta}$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"

      "exact_statement": "For the data of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:weight\">8</a>, with $s>0$, there is a projective contraction $f:N\\to C$ and an effective horizontal rational boundary $H$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$N$ is $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, its generic fibre is geometrically integral and birational to $V$, and $(N,T+H)$ is dlt, where $T=(f^*c)_{\\mathrm{red}}$;*\n\n2.  *$K_N+T+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q,C}0$;*\n\n\n3.  *for the same field-theoretic form $\\Omega$ and $w=w_z(\\phi)$, there is an equality of rational divisors near $T$, $$\\label{eq:prepared-equality}\n                        \\frac1m\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_N(\\Omega)+T+H=w f^*c.$$*\n\n*In particular, on the generic fibre, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mH_\\eta=0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-residue-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:(N,T+H)\\to C$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:prepared\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:prepared\">11</a>, with relative dimension $s\\ge1$, and suppose that $S$ is a coefficient-one component of $H$. Factor $S\\to C$ as $$S\\xrightarrow{h}C_S\\xrightarrow{\\nu}C,$$ where $h$ is a contraction and $\\nu$ is finite. Then $C_S$ is a smooth projective curve and $$\\deg\\nu\\le D_s$$ for an integer $D_s$ depending only on $s$. For every $c'\\in C_S$ above $c$, with ramification index $e$, there are data satisfying Theorem <a href=\"#thm:weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:weight\">8</a> in relative dimension $s-1$ and degree $m$ over $\\mathbb C(C_S)$, with a rational form $\\phi_S$ whose weight is $$\\label{eq:residue-weight}\n                             w_u(\\phi_S)=e w_z(\\phi)$$ for a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-residue-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:(N,T+H)\\to C$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:prepared\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:prepared\">11</a>, with relative dimension $s\\ge1$, and suppose that $S$ is a coefficient-one component of $H$. Factor $S\\to C$ as $$S\\xrightarrow{h}C_S\\xrightarrow{\\nu}C,$$ where $h$ is a contraction and $\\nu$ is finite. Then $C_S$ is a smooth projective curve and $$\\deg\\nu\\le D_s$$ for an integer $D_s$ depending only on $s$. For every $c'\\in C_S$ above $c$, with ramification index $e$, there are data satisfying Theorem <a href=\"#thm:weight\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:weight\">8</a> in relative dimension $s-1$ and degree $m$ over $\\mathbb C(C_S)$, with a rational form $\\phi_S$ whose weight is $$\\label{eq:residue-weight}\n                             w_u(\\phi_S)=e w_z(\\phi)$$ for a rational uniformizer $u$ at $c'$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-product-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B)$ be a projective klt pair over $\\mathbb C$ with $B\\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. There is a finite quasi-étale cover $\\pi:P\\to V$ and a decomposition $$(P,\\pi^*B)\n \\simeq (F,H)\\times A\\times\\prod_j Y_j\\times\\prod_k Z_k,$$ where $(F,H)$ is a rationally connected klt log Calabi–Yau pair, $A$ is an abelian variety, and the $Y_j$ and $Z_k$ are respectively irreducible Calabi–Yau and irreducible holomorphic symplectic varieties in the singular Beauville–Bogomolov decomposition. All factors are $\\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. Point factors are omitted, and the boundary is pulled back entirely from $F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rc-aut",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(F,H)$ be a rationally connected projective klt pair over $\\mathbb C$ with $H\\geq0$ and $K_F+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. Then $$H^j(F,\\mathcal O_F)=0\\quad(j>0),\\qquad\n H^0(F,\\Omega_F^{[1]})=0,\\qquad\n H^0\\bigl(F,\\mathcal T_F(-\\log\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}H)\\bigr)=0.$$ For every ample line bundle $L$, the group of automorphisms of $(F,H)$ preserving $L$ is finite.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-semilinear",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a characteristic-zero field, and let $P=\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i$ be a product of geometrically integral projective blocks as above. Let $H_P$ be the pullback of the boundary on its rationally connected block, or zero if that block is absent. Suppose $$\\rho:R\\longrightarrow P$$ is a finite quasi-étale morphism from a geometrically integral normal variety, and a finite group $G$ acts semilinearly on $(R,H_R)$, where $H_R=\\rho^*H_P$. Put $s=\\dim R>0$. There are normal geometrically integral varieties $R_i$, finite quasi-étale maps $R_i\\to P_i$, and a finite quasi-étale map $R\\to\\prod_iR_i$ such that every $g^{s!}$, for $g\\in G$, induces regular semilinear automorphisms of the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. Here $H_i$ is the pulled-back boundary on the rationally connected block and is zero on the other blocks. The projections from $R$ are equivariant for these automorphisms. Each geometric $R_i$ has the same block properties as $P_i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-semilinear",

Put $s=\\dim R>0$. Here $H_i$ is the pulled-back boundary on the rationally connected block and is zero on the other blocks. The projections from $R$ are equivariant for these automorphisms. Each geometric $R_i$ has the same block properties as $P_i$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-block-forms",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective curve $C$. Let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective $s$-fold pair over $K$, with $s>0$, geometrically klt, $B\\geq0$, and $K_V$ rational Cartier. Suppose that, for an integer $m>0$, a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$.*\n\n*There is a finite Galois extension $K_1/K$, a geometrically integral normal projective variety $R/K_1$, and finite quasi-étale maps $$\\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]\n R \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}R_i\n \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\n \\arrow[r] & V_{K_1}\n\\end{tikzcd}$$ with the following properties. The field $K_1(R)$ is finite Galois over $K(V)$; its Galois group acts semilinearly on $R$, maps onto $\\operatorname{Gal}(K_1/K)$, and preserves the pulled-back pair. For each element $g$ of this group, $g^{s!}$ induces regular semilinear actions on the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. There is at most one rationally connected block, chosen with the cover-stability of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:minimal-rc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:minimal-rc\">15</a>, and all other blocks are boundary-free of the types in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:product-cover\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:product-cover\">13</a>.*\n\n\n\n*Put $p_i=m$ on the rationally connected block and $p_i=1$ on the other blocks. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\\eta_i$ over $K_1$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:block-trivializations}\n                       \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\eta_i)+p_iH_i=0.$$ On $R$, after pulling back all forms, one has $$\\label{eq:block-product-form}\n                 \\phi=a\\bigwedge_{i\\in I}\\eta_i^{\\otimes m/p_i}\n                 \\qquad\\text{for some }a\\in K_1^*.$$ The wedge uses any fixed ordering of the blocks and the natural identification of their relative canonical lines.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-block-forms",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective curve $C$. Let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective $s$-fold pair over $K$, with $s>0$, geometrically klt, $B\\geq0$, and $K_V$ rational Cartier. Suppose that, for an integer $m>0$, a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$.*\n\n*There is a finite Galois extension $K_1/K$, a geometrically integral normal projective variety $R/K_1$, and finite quasi-étale maps $$\\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]\n R \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}R_i\n \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\n \\arrow[r] & V_{K_1}\n\\end{tikzcd}$$ with the following properties. The field $K_1(R)$ is finite Galois over $K(V)$; its Galois group acts semilinearly on $R$, maps onto $\\operatorname{Gal}(K_1/K)$, and preserves the pulled-back pair. For each element $g$ of this group, $g^{s!}$ induces regular semilinear actions on the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. There is at most one rationally connected block, chosen with the cover-stability of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:minimal-rc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:minimal-rc\">15</a>, and all other blocks are boundary-free of the types in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:product-cover\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:product-cover\">13</a>.*\n\n\n\n*Put $p_i=m$ on the rationally connected block and $p_i=1$ on the other blocks. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\\eta_i$ over $K_1$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:block-trivializations}\n                       \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\eta_i)+p_iH_i=0.$$ On $R$, after pulling back all forms, one has $$\\label{eq:block-product-form}\n                 \\phi=a\\bigwedge_{i\\in I}\\eta_i^{\\otimes m/p_i}\n                 \\qquad\\text{for some }a\\in K_1^*.$$ The wedge uses any fixed ordering of the blocks and the natural identification of their relative canonical lines.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective curve $C$. Let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective $s$-fold pair over $K$, with $s>0$, geometrically klt, $B\\geq0$, and $K_V$ rational Cartier. Suppose that, for an integer $m>0$, a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$.*\n\n*There is a finite Galois extension $K_1/K$, a geometrically integral normal projective variety $R/K_1$, and finite quasi-étale maps $$\\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]\n R \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}R_i\n \\arrow[r] & \\displaystyle\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\n \\arrow[r] & V_{K_1}\n\\end{tikzcd}$$ with the following properties. The field $K_1(R)$ is finite Galois over $K(V)$; its Galois group acts semilinearly on $R$, maps onto $\\operatorname{Gal}(K_1/K)$, and preserves the pulled-back pair. For each element $g$ of this group, $g^{s!}$ induces regular semilinear actions on the pairs $(R_i,H_i)$. There is at most one rationally connected block, chosen with the cover-stability of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:minimal-rc\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:minimal-rc\">15</a>, and all other blocks are boundary-free of the types in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:product-cover\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:product-cover\">13</a>.*\n\n\n\n*Put $p_i=m$ on the rationally connected block and $p_i=1$ on the other blocks. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\\eta_i$ over $K_1$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:block-trivializations}\n                       \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\eta_i)+p_iH_i=0.$$ On $R$, after pulling back all forms, one has $$\\label{eq:block-product-form}\n                 \\phi=a\\bigwedge_{i\\in I}\\eta_i^{\\otimes m/p_i}\n                 \\qquad\\text{for some }a\\in K_1^*.$$ The wedge uses any fixed ordering of the blocks and the natural identification of their relative canonical lines.",
      "explanation": "The comparison cover carries factor log trivializations whose product differs from the original form by a base scalar.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-marked-semistable",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C_1$ be a smooth projective complex curve with a marked point $c_1$, and let a finite cyclic group act on $C_1$, fixing $c_1$. Consider finitely many geometrically integral normal projective varieties over $\\mathbb C(C_1)$, each with a compatible semilinear action of this group and an invariant finite set of horizontal divisor markings. After a finite extension of curve fields and replacement by compatible finite-order lifts of the actions, there are equivariant smooth projective models for which the marked fibre is reduced simple normal crossings and its union with the horizontal markings is simple normal crossings. The generic fibres may be log resolutions. If a given generic fibre is already smooth and has no markings requiring resolution, it can be left unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normalized-product",
        "exact_quote": "Multiplying each $\\eta_i$ by an integral power of $u$, one can arrange $w_u(\\eta_i)=0$. For these normalized forms, the exterior product in common degree $m$ has weight zero.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-character",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $h\\geq0$ and $p\\geq1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-klt-weight",
        "exact_quote": "For every $s\\geq1$ and $m\\geq1$ there is a positive integer $q_{\\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)$ with the following property. Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective complex curve, let $z$ be a uniformizer at $c\\in C$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective geometrically klt $s$-fold pair over $K$. Assume that $K_V$ is rational Cartier, $B\\geq0$, and that a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$. Then $$q_{\\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)w_z(\\phi)\\in\\mathbb Z.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-klt-weight",
          "exact_quote": "For every $s\\geq1$ and $m\\geq1$ there is a positive integer $q_{\\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)$ with the following property. Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective complex curve, let $z$ be a uniformizer at $c\\in C$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective geometrically klt $s$-fold pair over $K$. Assume that $K_V$ is rational Cartier, $B\\geq0$, and that a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$. Then $$q_{\\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)w_z(\\phi)\\in\\mathbb Z.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $s\\geq1$ and $m\\geq1$ there is a positive integer $q_{\\mathrm{klt}}(s,m)$ with the following property. Let $K=\\mathbb C(C)$ for a smooth projective complex curve, let $z$ be a uniformizer at $c\\in C$, and let $(V,B)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective geometrically klt $s$-fold pair over $K$. Assume that $K_V$ is rational Cartier, $B\\geq0$, and that a rational $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB=0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $d\\geq 1$ and a finite set $\\Phi\\subset [0,1]\\cap\\mathbb Q$. There is an integer $p_0=p_0(d,\\Phi)>0$, clearing the denominators of $\\Phi$, with the following property. Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective varieties with $\\dim X\\leq d$, and let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\\geq0$ and nonzero coefficients in $\\Phi$. Suppose that $K_X+B$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $$K_X+B\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}f^*L$$ for a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$. Then there are $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C(X)^*$ and a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D_Z\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}L$ such that $$\\label{eq:exact-presentation}\n K_X+B+\\frac{1}{p_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\\geq0$, and suppose that $$K_X+B+\\frac1m\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ for an integer $m>0$, a function $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C(X)^*$, and a $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D_Z$. Let $\\tau:W\\to Z$ be a smooth projective birational model, and let $P$ be a prime divisor of $W$. Write $\\alpha=\\operatorname{coeff}_P(\\tau^*D_Z)$, and let $t_P$ be the threshold for the crepant induced sub-pair over the generic point of $P$. Put $s=\\dim X-\\dim Z$. Then there are a smooth projective curve $C$, a point $c\\in C$, a rational uniformizer $z$ at $c$, and a regular function-field extension of $\\mathbb C(C)$ admitting a geometrically integral projective normal effective $s$-fold pair $(V,B_V)$ that is geometrically lc, and a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\\phi$ such that $$\\label{eq:slice-weight}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\phi)+mB_V=0,\\qquad w_z(\\phi)=\\alpha-1+t_P.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rounded-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "All varieties in this statement are normal and projective over a field of characteristic zero.*\n\n\n1.  *Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction and let $D$ and $D_Z$ be $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors such that $$D+\\frac1a\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ for an integer $a>0$ and $\\psi\\in k(X)^*$. For every positive $\\ell$ divisible by $a$, the spaces of divisorial sections, regarded as rational functions, satisfy $$\\label{eq:section-pullback}\n     H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal O_X(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr)\n     =\\psi^{\\ell/a}f^*H^0\\bigl(Z,\\mathcal O_Z(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D_Z\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr).$$*\n\n2.  *Let $X\\dashrightarrow X'$ be birational, let $D$ and $D'$ be $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors, and suppose that on a common resolution $p:T\\to X$, $q:T\\to X'$ one has $$p^*D=q^*D'+E,$$ where $E\\geq0$ is $q$-exceptional. Under the identification of the function fields, for every integer $\\ell>0$ one has $$\\label{eq:section-birational}\n     H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal O_X(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr)\n     =H^0\\bigl(X',\\mathcal O_{X'}(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D'\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rounded-comparison",

          "exact_quote": "All varieties in this statement are normal and projective over a field of characteristic zero.*\n\n\n1.  *Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction and let $D$ and $D_Z$ be $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors such that $$D+\\frac1a\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z$$ for an integer $a>0$ and $\\psi\\in k(X)^*$. For every positive $\\ell$ divisible by $a$, the spaces of divisorial sections, regarded as rational functions, satisfy $$\\label{eq:section-pullback}\n     H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal O_X(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr)\n     =\\psi^{\\ell/a}f^*H^0\\bigl(Z,\\mathcal O_Z(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D_Z\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr).$$*\n\n2.  *Let $X\\dashrightarrow X'$ be birational, let $D$ and $D'$ be $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors, and suppose that on a common resolution $p:T\\to X$, $q:T\\to X'$ one has $$p^*D=q^*D'+E,$$ where $E\\geq0$ is $q$-exceptional. Under the identification of the function fields, for every integer $\\ell>0$ one has $$\\label{eq:section-birational}\n     H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal O_X(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr)\n     =H^0\\bigl(X',\\mathcal O_{X'}(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D'\\right\\rfloor)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.9"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-rounded-comparison"
    },

      "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and a finite set $\\Phi\\subset[0,1]\\cap\\mathbb Q$. There is an integer $N=N(d,\\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties with $\\dim X\\leq d$ and $\\dim Z>0$, and let $(X,B)$ be lc with $B\\geq0$, coefficients in $\\Phi$, and $D=K_X+B$ $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Suppose that $D\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}f^*L$ for a big $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$. Then $|\\left\\lfloor ND\\right\\rfloor|$ is nonempty, and its section ratios generate exactly $f^*\\mathbb C(Z)$ inside $\\mathbb C(X)$. This is also the field $K(D)$ generated by section ratios in all positive degrees.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\subset k'$ be an extension of algebraically closed fields, let $X$ be a normal integral projective variety over $k$, and let $D$ be a $\\mathbb Q$-divisor on $X$. Write $X'=X_{k'}$ and $D'=D_{k'}$. For each $\\ell>0$ having nonzero sections, let $F_\\ell\\subset k(X)$ be the field generated over $k$ by ratios of sections of $\\mathcal O_X(\\left\\lfloor \\ell D\\right\\rfloor)$, and define $F'_\\ell$ similarly on $X'$. Then $$F'_\\ell=k'F_\\ell,\\qquad K(D')=k'K(D),$$ where the composita are taken in $k'(X')$. In particular, for a fixed $m>0$, nonemptiness of $|\\left\\lfloor mD\\right\\rfloor|$ and the equality $F_m=K(D)$ hold if and only if they hold after extension to $k'$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Rounded degree section space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For an integer $\ell>0$, write $$V_\ell(D)=H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)\bigr).$$
- **Degree ratio field** ([source](#sec:introduction)): When $V_\ell(D)\neq0$, the ratios of its nonzero sections generate a field $F_\ell(D)\subset k(X)$.
- **Full Iitaka field** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $$K(D)=k\left(\frac{s}{t}: s,t\in V_\ell(D)\setminus\{0\},\ \ell>0,\ V_\ell(D)\neq0\right).$$
- **Contraction** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties satisfying $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$.
- **Rational uniformizer** ([source](#sec:weights)): A rational uniformizer at $c$ is an element $z\in K$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_c z=1$.
- **Relative pluricanonical form** ([source](#sec:weights)): A rational relative $m$-canonical form is a nonzero element of $(\bigwedge^s\Omega_{F/K})^{\otimes m}$.
- **Quasi-étale morphism** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A finite surjective morphism between normal varieties is *quasi-étale* if it is étale in codimension one.
- **Semilinear automorphism** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A *semilinear automorphism* of a variety over a field $k$ is an automorphism together with an automorphism of $k$ over which it lies; equivalently, it is a $k$-isomorphism to the corresponding field conjugate.
- **Curve weight** ([source](#stmt-def-weight)): For a rational relative $m$-canonical form $\phi$, set $$\label{eq:weight-definition} w_z(\phi)=\inf_E \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(\phi\wedge(dz/z)^{\otimes m}\bigr)+m} {m\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E z}.$$ Here $E$ runs through the normalized divisorial valuations of $F/\mathbb C$ whose restriction to $K$ is a positive multiple of $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_c$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-prepared) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prepared): Relative minimal models -> Prepared dlt weight identity
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue-reduction): Pluriresidue and bounded Stein constants -> Lower-dimensional weight problem
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-semilinear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-semilinear): Boundary-preserving tangent endomorphisms and Stein factorization -> Regular factor actions after a bounded power
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalized-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalized-product): Equivariant marked semistable reduction and logarithmic products -> Normalized product weight
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-other-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-other-characters): Coherent Lefschetz and normal-component residues -> Bounded nonabelian factor characters
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-klt-weight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-klt-weight): Character cancellation -> Klt weight denominator
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-weight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue-reduction-2): Induction through residue or the klt bound -> Uniform curve-weight denominator
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-presentation): Normal index and same-degree descent -> Uniform exact principal presentation
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-moduli-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slice-2): Transverse slicing and integral coefficients on smooth determinations -> Uniform nef Cartier moduli multiple
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-effective-fibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-effective-fibration): Polarized effective birationality and complete rounded section comparison -> Uniform system generating the entire base ratio field
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-descent-2): Good minimal models, rounded comparison and field descent -> Uniform generation of the full Iitaka field

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-IITAKA**: A uniform complete rounded pluricanonical system is nonempty and generates the full Iitaka field inside the original function field.
- **I-WEIGHT**: An exact relative log trivialization has a curve weight cleared by an integer depending only on relative dimension and form degree.
- **I-MODULI**: A uniform multiple of the normalized moduli trace is nef Cartier on every smooth projective determination.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-DEGREE**: Use an arbitrary chosen degree to compute the full Iitaka field. -> Existence of a uniform degree does not identify the chosen degree.
- **A-HYPOTHESES**: Apply the Iitaka-field conclusion when a pair or adjoint hypothesis fails. -> The field equality lies outside the stated regime.
- **A-PRINCIPAL**: Keep the denominator bound after arbitrary rational principal shifts of the moduli representative. -> The shift can introduce new denominators.
- **A-COVER**: Treat bounded normalized character order as a bound on covering degree or entire automorphism order. -> The cancellation controls the character without controlling these larger orders.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The source states that the bound is existential. The uniform pluricanonical degree and moduli denominator rely on companion existence theorems and are not supplied as finite constructive procedures. Arithmetic identities in the proof do not compute the uniform degree or certify the Iitaka-field equality for arbitrary projective lc pairs.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-main-regime** (hypothesis): The uniform Iitaka-degree theorem requires fixed dimension $d\geq5$ and a fixed finite rational coefficient set. Blocked use: ['Do not extend this theorem to unrestricted coefficient sets or dimensions outside its stated range.']
- **L-main-geometry** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires an algebraically closed ground field of characteristic zero and a normal integral projective log canonical pair. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the theorem to positive characteristic, nonnormal varieties, or pairs whose singularity assumptions are unverified.']
- **L-main-adjoint** (hypothesis): Effectiveness, allowed boundary coefficients, a rational Cartier adjoint, and nonnegative Kodaira dimension are required together. Blocked use: ['Do not assume applicability when any adjoint or boundary hypothesis is false or unknown.']
- **L-existential** (nonconstructive): The uniform degree is existential; a computable numerical degree and an executable construction are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not substitute a guessed degree or claim a certified runtime for computing the Iitaka field.']
- **L-field-equality** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns the complete rounded section system and equality of subfields inside the original function field. Blocked use: ['Do not replace the complete system by an arbitrary subsystem or report only an abstract isomorphism of fields.']
- **L-normalization** (hypothesis): The degree fixing the exact canonical bundle presentation must be retained when using the moduli-denominator bound. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the denominator bound to an arbitrarily shifted rational principal representative.']
- **L-moduli-model** (hypothesis): The moduli denominator conclusion is for smooth projective determinations of the moduli b-divisor. Blocked use: ['Do not infer the same Cartier conclusion on an arbitrary singular model or for an unrelated divisor.']
- **L-moduli-ground** (hypothesis): The moduli theorem uses complex projective contractions with positive-dimensional base and the stated log canonical boundary and adjoint hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer this moduli theorem to another ground field or omit the contraction and boundary conditions without a separate justification.']
- **L-weight-trivialization** (hypothesis): The weight denominator requires an exact log trivialization of an effective geometrically log canonical generic pair; the form degree already constrains the boundary. Blocked use: ['Do not use the coefficient independence of the weight bound for arbitrary forms or nonintegral multiples of the boundary.']
- **L-cover-order** (formal_scope): Bounded normalized character orders do not bound the factor cover degrees or the orders of the entire automorphisms. Blocked use: ['Do not use character bounds as a bound for the whole cover or its automorphism group.']
- **L-rounded-divisibility** (hypothesis): The pullback comparison of rounded section spaces requires the comparison degree to be divisible by the exact presentation degree. Blocked use: ['Do not invoke the displayed pullback identity at an arbitrary nondivisible degree.']
- **L-companion-input** (formal_scope): The proof depends on the companion good-model, normal-index, and arithmetic Stein-degree inputs in their stated regimes. Blocked use: ['Do not present the proof as independent of these existence and boundedness inputs.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For normal projective log canonical pairs over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero, of fixed dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 5 and with effective boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set, we prove that one complete rounded pluricanonical system generates the full Iitaka field whenever the ℚ-Cartier log canonical divisor has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. Its degree depends only on the dimension and coefficient set. For the paper's normalized canonical bundle formulae over ℂ, we also bound the Cartier denominators of the moduli divisors on smooth projective determining models in terms of the dimension and coefficient set.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $(X,B)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic zero, and put $D=K_X+B$. When $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$, the sections of multiples of $D$ determine the Iitaka fibration. The effective problem asks whether one degree, depending only on the dimension and the allowed boundary coefficients, already determines that fibration. A uniform degree must control both the birational geometry of its base and the information lost in passing between different pluricanonical degrees.

We formulate the conclusion directly as an equality of subfields of the function field. For an integer $\ell>0$, write $$V_\ell(D)=H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)\bigr).$$ When $V_\ell(D)\neq0$, the ratios of its nonzero sections generate a field $F_\ell(D)\subset k(X)$. Define $$K(D)=k\left(\frac{s}{t}:
       s,t\in V_\ell(D)\setminus\{0\},\
       \ell>0,\ V_\ell(D)\neq0\right).$$ Thus $F_\ell(D)$ is the function field of the image of the complete degree-$\ell$ system, viewed inside $k(X)$, and $K(D)$ collects these fields over all degrees. The reflexive sheaf $\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor \ell D\right\rfloor)$ makes this definition meaningful even when $\ell D$ is not Cartier.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Uniform log Iitaka degree). *For every integer $d\geq5$ and every finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $m=m(d,\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $k$ be any algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B)$ be a normal integral projective lc $d$-fold pair over $k$. Assume that $B\geq0$, every nonzero coefficient of $B$ belongs to $\Phi$, $D=K_X+B$ is rational Cartier, and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$. Then $$V_m(D)\neq0,\qquad F_m(D)=K(D)
                       \quad\text{inside }k(X).$$*

</div>

The bound is existential. The proof uses the good-model theorem of <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA)], the normal log canonical index theorem of <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[U](#ref-U)], and the arithmetic Stein-degree theorem of <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[SD](#ref-SD)]. Their precise statements are recalled in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>. The contribution developed here is the passage from these inputs to uniform denominators for log fibrations with horizontal boundary, followed by the exact comparison of rounded section fields. We state the higher-dimensional range $d\geq5$ to complement the lower-dimensional results discussed below; the intermediate arguments are formulated in all fibre dimensions.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and the denominator problem

Iitaka’s work established the asymptotic fibration associated with pluricanonical systems <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Iitaka](#ref-Iitaka)]. Uniform effectivity asks for a degree independent of the particular variety. In the general-type case this became uniform birationality of pluricanonical maps, proved in arbitrary dimension by Hacon–McKernan, Takayama, and Tsuji <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[HaconMcKernan](#ref-HaconMcKernan), [Takayama](#ref-Takayama), [Tsuji](#ref-Tsuji)]. Hacon–McKernan–Xu extended effective birationality to big log canonical adjoints with coefficients in a DCC set <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HMX](#ref-HMX), Theorem 1.3]. That theorem includes round-down systems, the convention used here.

When the Kodaira dimension is smaller than the dimension, the induced log adjoint on the generic fibre has Kodaira dimension zero. The canonical bundle formula transfers the adjoint to the base, where the variation of the fibres contributes a moduli divisor. Fujino–Mori developed this approach to effective Iitaka fibrations <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FujinoMori](#ref-FujinoMori)]; Viehweg–Zhang obtained effective results with a surface base and controlled fibre invariants <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ViehwegZhang](#ref-ViehwegZhang)]. Birkar–Zhang’s general effectivity theorem depends on the dimension, the least nonvanishing pluricanonical degree of the general fibre, and the middle Betti number of a smooth model of its canonical cover <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BZ](#ref-BZ), Theorem 1.2]. Their effective birationality theorem for polarized pairs <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BZ](#ref-BZ), Theorem 1.3] is also the final birational input in the present proof.

The logarithmic problem introduces horizontal boundary on the generic fibre. Earlier results include the low-dimensional klt results of Todorov and Todorov–Xu <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Todorov](#ref-Todorov), [TodorovXu](#ref-TodorovXu)], and the boundedness and birationality results of Hacon–Xu for klt pairs whose boundary is big over the generic point of the Iitaka base <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HaconXu](#ref-HaconXu)]. Chen–Han–Liu formulate the effective log Iitaka problem for lc pairs with DCC coefficients and relate it to good minimal models and complements <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ChenHanLiu](#ref-ChenHanLiu), Conjecture 1.2 and Theorem 1.4]. They obtain the dimension-at-most-three case in <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ChenHanLiu](#ref-ChenHanLiu), Corollary 1.5]. Our coefficient set is finite, and the higher-dimensional argument here uses the three companion theorems stated above.

A bounded index of the generic fibre does not by itself give the needed Cartier denominator for the moduli divisor. After a curve base change one can obtain semistable reduction, but its ramification degree is generally uncontrolled. The relevant question is whether the action of inertia on a suitably normalized logarithmic volume form has bounded order. Only this character needs to be bounded; the covering degree and the order of the entire automorphism need not be.

Fujino–Mori already control finite characters through the cohomology of the fibre <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FujinoMori](#ref-FujinoMori), Theorem 3.1 and Sections 3.6–3.8]. The companion <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[U](#ref-U)] develops the character method for boundary-free factors using the singular Beauville–Bogomolov decomposition. The curve and effective-system arguments of <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)] treat log Calabi–Yau fibrations in total dimension at most four, using the index of the whole reduced slc fibre. We adapt these methods to horizontal boundary in arbitrary fibre dimension. The geometric structure theorem of Matsumura–Wang <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[MW](#ref-MW), Theorem 1.3] supplies a rationally connected factor carrying the boundary, together with abelian and nonabelian Beauville–Bogomolov factors. The additional work is to retain the factor decomposition under semilinear actions and to control the logarithmic character of the rationally connected factor.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The main intermediate result and the proof

The reusable intermediate statement is a bound for weights over curves. Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve, put $K=\mathbb C(C)$, let $z$ be a uniformizer at $c\in C$, and let $(V,\Delta)$ be a geometrically integral normal projective lc $s$-fold pair over $K$, with $\Delta\geq0$. For a positive integer $a$, a degree-$a$ rational log trivialization is a nonzero rational $a$-canonical form $\phi$ satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\phi)+a\Delta=0$. Its weight at $c$ is the infimum $$w_z(\phi)=\inf_E
 \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(\phi\wedge(dz/z)^{\otimes a}\bigr)+a}
      {a\,\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E z},$$ where $E$ ranges over divisorial valuations of the total function field centred over $c$. These are ordinary canonical orders; the additive $a$ records the logarithmic pole along the base. Theorem <a href="#thm:weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:weight">8</a> proves that an integer depending only on $s$ and $a$ clears this weight. Once a degree-$a$ trivialization is given, no further coefficient parameter is needed.

There are two parts to the weight argument. If a dlt model has a horizontal coefficient-one component, taking a rational pluriresidue reduces the fibre dimension. Its Stein factorization may change the curve field. The arithmetic bound of <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[SD](#ref-SD)] controls that change, so residue gives an induction with bounded denominators.

In the klt case the product theorem of <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MW](#ref-MW)] applies, but the product cover need not carry the inertia action. We pass to a Galois comparison cover and prove that a bounded power acts separately on suitable finite covers of the factors. The proof uses the algebra of endomorphisms of the tangent sheaf of the comparison cover that preserve its subsheaf of vector fields tangent to the boundary. The required vanishing of global vector fields tangent to the boundary on the rationally connected factor follows from a finite invariant log-volume measure.

After equivariant semistable reduction, each factor form can be normalized to have weight zero. Their product has weight zero as well. If the original form differs from this product by a base function $v$ on a curve cover, and $c'$ is a point over $c$ of ramification index $e$, then $$e\,w_z(\phi)=\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{c'}(v)}{a}.$$ The inertia identity forces $e$ to divide a bounded multiple of $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{c'}(v)$ once the factor characters have bounded order. This cancels the uncontrolled ramification and bounds the original weight denominator.

For abelian factors the character acts on a bounded-rank integral cohomology group. For the other factors we instead use a dlt special fibre on a good model. Du Bois base change determines its structure-sheaf cohomology, and coherent Lefschetz gives a fixed point of a bounded power of the action. A bounded further power preserves a normal component. The residue there is a log trivialization in the original degree, so its different has coefficients in a fixed finite set. The normal lc index theorem of <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[U](#ref-U)], applied to the cyclic quotient of this component, bounds the character. This step works on one normal component and is the reason an index theorem for a reducible special fibre is unnecessary.

To finish the proof over $\mathbb C$, let $f:Y\to Z$ be the semiample contraction of a good model $(Y,B_Y)$ of $(X,B)$. The normal index theorem gives a bounded positive integer $p_0$ and a rational function $\psi\in\mathbb C(Y)^*$ such that the canonical bundle formula has the exact presentation $$K_Y+B_Y+\frac1{p_0}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*(K_Z+B_Z+M_Z).$$ Here $B_Z$ is the discriminant defined by log canonical thresholds, and the equality specifies the moduli representative $M_Z$. Fixing the degree $p_0$ is essential: arbitrary rational principal shifts can introduce new denominators. Slicing the base by a curve identifies a coefficient of this moduli divisor with the curve weight, up to an integer. This proves Theorem <a href="#thm:moduli-denominator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:moduli-denominator">26</a>: a uniform multiple of the moduli trace on a suitable smooth birational model of $Z$ is nef Cartier.

Birkar–Zhang’s theorem then gives a birational system on the base. The remaining step is to identify complete rounded section spaces upstairs with those downstairs. This yields equality with $K(D)$ inside the original function field, including ratios initially appearing in other degrees, and permits descent from $\mathbb C$ to any algebraically closed characteristic-zero field.

Section <a href="#sec:mmp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mmp">3</a> records the relative minimal-model construction. Section <a href="#sec:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weights">4</a> proves the residue reduction for weights. Sections <a href="#sec:blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:blocks">5</a> and <a href="#sec:degeneration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:degeneration">6</a> establish the klt weight bound. Section <a href="#sec:fibration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fibration">7</a> converts that bound into moduli denominators, and Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">8</a> proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Conventions and companion theorems

We assume familiarity with discrepancies, singularities of pairs, and the minimal model program, as in <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[KM](#ref-KM), [KollarSing](#ref-KollarSing)]. We recall the conventions and precise theorem inputs needed for the argument. The argument is carried out over $\mathbb C$ until the final descent to an arbitrary algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Varieties are integral and projective unless a different setting is specified. A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties satisfying $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$. In characteristic zero its generic fibre is geometrically integral. All boundaries in our argument are rational.

For a normal variety, a rational section of a divisorial sheaf is identified with a rational function. Thus, for a rational Weil divisor $D$, <a id="eq:section-inequality"></a>$$\label{eq:section-inequality}
 H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(\left\lfloor D\right\rfloor))
 =\{a\in k(X):\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(a)+D\geq0\}\cup\{0\}.$$ The equality uses the integrality of the orders of $a$. It does not require $\left\lfloor D\right\rfloor$ to be Cartier. We use compatible canonical divisors on birational models, obtained from one rational top differential. A rational $m$-canonical form means an element of the $m$th tensor power of the top differential line of a function field; its divisor is computed on normal models at codimension-one regular points.

We use log discrepancies. If $\pi:Y\to X$ is a birational model and $K_Y+B_Y=\pi^*(K_X+B)$, then the log discrepancy of a prime divisor $E$ on $Y$ is $1-\operatorname{coeff}_E B_Y$. In particular, log canonicity means that all these numbers are nonnegative. Crepant boundaries on higher models may have negative coefficients. Effectiveness will always be specified when it is needed. We use dlt adjunction in its usual form: a component $S$ of the coefficient-one part of a dlt boundary is normal, and $$(K_Y+B_Y)|_S=K_S+\operatorname{Diff}_S(B_Y-S)$$ with an effective lc different when $B_Y$ is an effective boundary <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KM](#ref-KM), [KollarSing](#ref-KollarSing)].

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The three companion inputs

The following statements are the substantive inputs from companion manuscripts. We recall their full scope because the coefficient and ground-field conditions matter at different places in the proof.

<a id="stmt-thm-normal-index"></a>
<div id="thm:normal-index" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Normal log canonical indices <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[U](#ref-U), Theorem 1.2]). *For every integer $n\geq0$ and every DCC set $\Psi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $a(n,\Psi)>0$ such that every normal integral projective lc pair $(V,\Delta)$ over $\mathbb C$ with $\dim V=n$, $\Delta\geq0$, coefficients in $\Psi$, and $K_V+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ satisfies $$a(n,\Psi)(K_V+\Delta)\sim0.$$ Here the displayed multiple is an integral principal divisor.*

</div>

A DCC set is a set with no infinite strictly decreasing sequence. We will apply Theorem <a href="#thm:normal-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:normal-index">2</a> both to a finite coefficient set and to the DCC set produced by finite cyclic quotients. Its principal-divisor conclusion supplies an actual rational pluriform in a bounded degree.

<a id="stmt-thm-good-models"></a>
<div id="thm:good-models" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Good models <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Theorem 11.1]). *Every projective lc pair $(V,\Delta)$ over $\mathbb C$ with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoint $K_V+\Delta$ has a good log minimal model.*

</div>

We use only the rational-boundary case, including semiampleness of a nef adjoint. This theorem supplies existence; the terminating programs used below also use the MMP with scaling <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[TX](#ref-TX)]. Section <a href="#sec:mmp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mmp">3</a> explains precisely how to apply these results over a projective curve.

<a id="stmt-thm-stein-degree"></a>
<div id="thm:stein-degree" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Arithmetic Stein degrees <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[SD](#ref-SD), Main Theorem]). *For every integer $n\geq1$ and every real number $t>0$, there is an integer $N(n,t)$ with the following property. Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, and let $(V,\Delta)$ be a normal integral projective lc rational pair over $F$ with $$\dim V=n,\qquad H^0(V,\mathcal O_V)=F,\qquad
 \Delta\geq0,\qquad K_V+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0.$$ If $S$ is a prime component of $\Delta$ of coefficient at least $t$, the algebraic closure of $F$ in $F(S)$ has degree at most $N(n,t)$ over $F$.*

</div>

For a proper normal component this algebraic closure is its field of global functions. In our application $F$ is a complex curve field and $t=1$; the theorem controls the finite curve in the Stein factorization of a horizontal coefficient-one component.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Trivializations and constants

Two elementary facts will be useful repeatedly. First, a rational function with zero divisor on a normal integral proper variety is an invertible global function. Second, the degree of a geometric trivialization does not increase on descending the constants.

<a id="stmt-lem-trivialization-descent"></a>
<div id="lem:trivialization-descent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Descent in the same degree). *Let $V$ be a geometrically integral normal projective variety over a characteristic-zero field $F$. Let $D$ be an integral Weil divisor. If $D_{\overline F}$ is principal, then $D$ is principal. In particular, an integral principal multiple of a rational log canonical divisor on $V_{\overline F}$ descends in the same degree to $V$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-trivialization-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trivialization-descent</summary>

*Proof.* The divisorial sheaf $\mathcal O_V(D)$ becomes the trivial line bundle after extension to $\overline F$. Divisorial sheaves commute with this extension, as can be seen on a regular big open and by reflexive extension. Faithfully flat descent makes $\mathcal O_V(D)$ invertible. Proper base change shows that its space of sections is one-dimensional over $F$. The evaluation map from this space tensored with $\mathcal O_V$ is an isomorphism after scalar extension, hence already an isomorphism.
